## L12-107 — Documentation lease dispatch and Land admission (Doer delivery, 2026-10-22)

### Built and reused

Input HEAD `6c5c821eae23c8989b8e6dad638eae19e3b0c4c6` differs from `8b20f5185` only by the slice-2 brief. All implementation and test inputs therefore have the same baseline bytes as `8b20f5185`. Read the committed brief, design §3/§6, reuse check, L12-94/105/106, reading map, and relevant runner/Workflow admission contracts before implementation. History search found `leaseAdmission` introduced by `379e44239`; no older dispatch-gate implementation was found in the four target files.

- `bind()` calls the existing production `leaseAdmission` before seat policy, so even the inline fallback cannot swallow its whole-call refusal. It strips any path filter from dispatch context: a read-only Unit is refused too. The named held reason remains the module's `documentation-cutover-lease`; corrupt authority retains the module's separate fail-closed reason.
- `runUnitBody` calls that same gate before attempt sweeping, config bootstrap, admission-port calls, custody, controller locks or unit-directory creation, including resume. Its existing binding context now carries `lease: {repoRoot, worktreePath}` for every seat/fallback. The gate re-reads authority rather than trusting a cached grant.
- `prepareWorkflowRun` reaches the gate through `resolveStart`, before config, parent-frame charge or ledger creation. Placing it in that shared resolver also protects seat preflight before it can bootstrap config.
- `landRunnerItem` calls the same gate after the existing Work checkpoint and pinned-ref reads, but before `declareLandFiles`, Land port creation, candidate creation or Land WAL. Code-only lands and holder lands proceed. Existing `inspectGit`/`compareCommits`/paged `diffPaths` supply paths; no Git mutation mechanism was added.
- Owning boundary tests were extended in `bind.test.mjs`, `run-case-admission.test.mjs`, `merge-land.test.mjs`, and `case-admission-port.test.mjs`. Neither candidate `case-admission*.test.mjs` previously called `prepareWorkflowRun`; its new boundary proof belongs in the port/integration file, not the pure predicate file. Every held fixture uses production `acquireDocumentationCutoverLease`, not a fabricated sidecar or injected callback.

### Decisions inside the approved design

1. **Context carries checkout coordinates, not a bearer-token copy.** The lease module alone reads/hashes the token file in that checkout; workers use the existing working checkout. No token is added to argv, env, Unit records or binding provenance. Tests deliberately separate host cwd from holder checkout to observe context propagation. No boolean/cached-holder shortcut is accepted.
2. **Preserve read-only operation outside Git.** Run/Workflow use the existing `resolveGitRoots(..., {allowNoRepository:true})`; an explicit `worktree` is checked independently of cwd, so starting outside Git cannot evade the gate on a held target checkout. `bind` ignores only the module's explicit `not-a-repository` precondition error; every other inspection failure still propagates. No environment bypass was introduced.
3. **Use actual source paths for Land.** The old `changedFiles(repoRoot,item)` derives `fgw/<id>` and can return `[]` for a valid explicit `branch`. Admission now computes the merge-base diff of the exact pinned `branch`/`target` pair and reuses those paths for post-land reporting. Rename detection is explicitly off, so both old and new sides count as deletion/addition; deletes and type changes also count. Tests cover an item id different from the branch, scoped addition, sidecar, rename out, rename in, deletion and file-to-symlink change.
4. **Keep checkpoint ordering intact.** Work's existing `.fgos` checkpoint remains outside Land and before target pinning. The refusal guarantees no Land candidate/WAL; it does not claim to suppress Work's independently permitted code/state-only checkpoint.
5. **One proof per boundary.** The existing lease-module tests own token/corruption/scope policy cases. New tests prove gate placement and holder context; the existing read-only Unit completion test still owns successful worker completion. The new holder run proof asserts an actual bound worker attempt, not successful completion: this sandbox's attempt exits 127, as do corresponding existing baseline worker tests. No existing assertion was removed or weakened.
6. **No new configuration or infrastructure.** Existing native-fixture staging and test-only root controls are reused. The lease module, hooks, registry, doctor, skills, docs, other tests and baselines are unchanged. No real lease was acquired on this repository and no Git write or fgOS CLI command was run against it.

### Tests first and verification commands

New gate tests were written before production edits. Their initial single-file, name-filtered runs all exited **1**: bind reached a throwing seat-config Proxy; run reached main-checkout policy rather than lease refusal; Workflow created a run; the first merge fixture attempt exposed a fixture error (its unrestricted `git add -A` tracked and then removed its activation fence). The fixture now ignores only its local `.fgos/` through `.git/info/exclude`; it does not bypass a production check. Merge gate cases subsequently pass through real acquisition and Land. A second tests-first pair for an explicit held worktree with cwd outside Git exited **1**, then passed after checking the explicit worktree roots.

The prescribed bare single-file command was attempted on all six files before edits:

```sh
env -u CLAUDE_CODE_SESSION_ID FGOS_HOST_BIN=/home/vantt/.local/state/fgos/releases/sha256:9508f457d1ec11814de34149c6b19ce01ffbf237060ee6093fbaf442fac4b511/bin/fgos node --test <file>
```

Observed exits: bind **1**, run-case-admission **1**, merge-land **1**, case-admission-port **1**, run **1**, architecture **0**. The sandbox makes the default global state root and `/var/tmp` read-only. A second pass using writable test roots still exposed missing native-payload staging (and an initially group-writable scratch state root). The scratch state root was corrected to mode 0700; no production permission check was skipped.

Final complete-file commands used the same installed host input plus existing fixture/state-root controls and the existing production native staging helper:

## L12-108 — Hook cost, owner repair and holder proof (Lead under A42; Doer delivery, 2026-10-22)

### Supplied Lead rulings

Recorded from `step-06b-slice1-rework3-brief.md`, not a new owner decision or independent acceptance:

1. The reference hook has a POSIX sh front end: buffer transaction stdin once, exit without Node outside `prepared` or without an exact `refs/heads/main` update. Node/import failures affect main updates only. Measure a no-lease branch's five commits and five-commit rebase against no hook.
2. Doctor is not applicable unless this is a Git repository shipping `.githooks/reference-transaction`; consumer projects with their own pre-commit hooks must not become red.
3. A main-50 borrower is not holder proof. A legal held→released transition also needs the committing checkout's token to hash to the old holder hash. Held→abandoned is an **owner door by design in this single-owner repository**: the new sidecar must contain an abandon record with explicit owner confirmation and a non-empty written reason. The hook does not recompute drift. **Phase 9 cutover must recompute scope digests itself from Git and must never trust the sidecar's `drift` or `releaseScopeDigests` as authority.**
4. Confirmed owner abandon with a written reason is the only repair door for corrupt committed sidecars. Every other transition and ordinary commit keeps refusing on corrupt authority.
5. A failed transition restores the main checkout sidecar to the current `refs/heads/main` bytes, or removes it if absent, and removes a freshly written acquisition token.
6. Keep the persistent holder token in its ignored checkout-root `.fgos/`. `git clean -fdX` deletes it; only owner abandon recovers. No alternative token store or recovery mechanism is added.
7. Holder cutover edits reach main **only through `landRunnerItem`**, whose existing slice-2 gate authenticates the source checkout token. Raw main ref movement into frozen scope remains refused for holders too. No raw-Git holder bypass is introduced here.
8. Use each root's own first tree entry instead of paging its whole subtree; remove the redundant default-sidecar scope check.

### Implementation, reuse and limits

Input HEAD: `10a6b66cc`. Read the complete r3 review and brief. History searches found the old shell hook at `6aa3d5c19`, intentionally replaced at `8b20f5185` for NUL-safe Node policy. Reuse its **phase/ref shell filtering only**, retaining the newer shared Node parser, borrower check and byte-safe path policy. The existing `.githooks/pre-commit` is now the Node entry for both hooks, with one shared read-only Git adapter; the reference shell delegates to it in explicit reference-transaction mode. No new hook file, source-layer raw Git adapter, Git port, token environment variable or argv credential was added. Its ordinary pre-commit path and all non-lease guards are unchanged.

`legalTransition` now authenticates release with the committing checkout's token, and validates the owner's abandon record separately. Corrupt authority reaches only that same sidecar-only, direct-parent, borrowed owner-abandon edge. Repair writes a valid abandoned sidecar; because the corrupt holder identity cannot be trusted, its replacement hash is freshly generated without writing a token. The shared parser remains unchanged.

**Main commit versus linked holder:** ScopedCommit still commits in main under documentation-cutover-10 → main-50. For linked-holder release, the production door authenticates the caller's root token, then lends those already-authenticated bytes to main's existing token path for the synchronous commit, so both hooks can independently check the committing checkout. A `finally` restores main's previous token bytes or removes that temporary copy; the holder's persistent token is removed only after a successful commit. There is no new credential path or transport. Success and forced-commit-failure tests verify both cleanups, including preservation of an unrelated prior main token. This is cleanup for returned/throwing calls, **not a SIGKILL or power-loss cleanup guarantee**; no new crash-recovery mechanism is claimed.

The existing atomic sidecar writer is factored into one byte writer reused for token lending and byte-exact rollback; the existing Inspect sidecar read is factored for rollback without parsing/reserializing corrupt evidence. Rollback re-reads current main rather than restoring the preflight snapshot. Scope digests request only the first matching entry for each root and compare its exact path, preserving explicit absent roots. Inspect's internal recursive listing is unchanged; this removes repeated **paging**, not all backend tree traversal. The scope matcher still accepts optional custom sidecars without checking the default sidecar twice.

No slice-2 source/test file, setup registration, baseline, skill, or `docs/**` file was edited. Census regeneration also refreshes the four already-committed slice-2 source digests; those source files themselves are unchanged. The scanner reports **53 undeclared sites** (52 spawn + 1 unresolved binary), not a green one-door result. Sharing the hook adapter removes the duplicate reference-hook site from the prior 54; the remaining hook adapter is still reported, alongside the inherited script sites. No scanner rule or allowlist was weakened.

## L12-109 — Explicit caller checkout at execution and Land doors (Lead under A42; Doer delivery)

### Supplied ruling and scope

Recorded from `step-06b-slice2-rework-brief.md`, not a new owner decision or independent acceptance:

1. Holder means **can read the token in the explicitly named caller checkout**. This is the accepted single-user, accident-prevention trust model, not protection against deliberate bypass. No ambient-cwd fallback belongs in seat choice.
2. Admit at execution and Land doors before their effects, not in `bind()`: Unit run, Workflow start/resume/answer (including detached forms), runner Land and Workflow Land. Reuse the operation's admission across Units, seat walks and assignment replay.
3. Store a Workflow's origin in its existing run record; generated `wf/` checkouts never become its holder identity. Later operations use the recorded origin. A holder Workflow can integrate onto main. This amends L12-108 item 7's runner-Land-only wording to include **both sanctioned Land doors**; raw ref moves remain refused.
4. Runner Land takes a caller-checkout option. Approve, sync-root and promote pass the CLI's own checkout separately from the destination/main repository.
5. Consult the sidecar once per operation and measure the no-lease cost; no per-attempt consultations.
6. Corrupt authority keeps refusing execution and Land. The module's direct owner-abandon repair door stays outside `bind`/`runUnit`.
7. Correct the stale root-resolution/reclamation comment.

Input HEAD: `820385775a5dc626fabd3e6d494914f4fc9fe2eb`. The expanded assignment also authorizes `src/verbs/merge/promote-to-component.mjs` and `test/runner/promote-engine.test.mjs`; both are used. All named production paths exist at their supplied locations. No other code/test write was needed.

Read the full brief/review, L12-107/108, reading map and relevant Workflow/Execution/Seat Binding contracts before editing. History search (`git log -S'leaseAdmission'` and caller-checkout/worktree `-G` searches) found the gates introduced by `e8c1df31c`, not an older origin/admission mechanism to restore. Reused the existing lease reader/token policy, Workflow ledger and `case.opened`, Inspect diffs, native Land and existing fixture/native staging doors. The review's `scratchpad/6b2-review/repro.mjs` and `edge.mjs` are absent from **both** this worktree and `/home/vantt/projects/forgentX`; read attempts returned ENOENT. The committed review's scenarios were reconstructed in the owning production-path tests below, not represented as runs of those missing scripts.

### Implementation and decisions inside the ruling

- `bind()` no longer imports or consults the documentation lease. Dry binding, doctor and assignment replay only choose seats. `assignment-runner.mjs` needs no edit: its existing replay already calls that now-pure boundary.
- `admitDocumentationExecution` consolidates the execution-door check in `run.mjs`, requiring a declared checkout and catching **only** the lease module's typed `not-a-repository` answer. Other inspection errors and corrupt sidecars fail closed. Run calls it before sweep/config/reservation/custody/controller/unit creation. The entry passes its cwd and named worktree explicitly.
- Workflow start admits before config, parent-frame charging, ledger/advance/spawn or generated worktrees. CLI `--seats` admits before its potentially config-writing preflight and passes that same result into start. Standalone dry seat choice has no lease gate. Foreground start passes the operation-local result through the proposer and every writing/recovery Unit; no credential or persistent grant is added to Unit/assignment state.
- Origin is `case.opened.payload.callerCheckout` in the existing run's ledger, written by both start forms, not a second store or a change to guide/schema authority. Resume/answer read that record **before** acquiring advance, repairing a ledger tail, writing an answer round or spawning a child. Changing later `--worktree`/cwd cannot replace the recorded origin. A detached child is a **new operation**, so it refreshes admission before acting. A legacy/prepared-only run with no opened-origin record conservatively uses its main repository, not a later caller's token-bearing checkout; no retroactive privilege upgrade is inferred.
- `landRunnerItem(..., {callerCheckout})` authenticates that checkout before its checkpoint, native Land port, candidate or WAL. The existing checkpoint may advance the target; trusted-file resolution is refreshed afterwards without another lease consultation. Approve's two call sites, sync-root and the complete promote use-case → engine → Land chain pass the CLI cwd independently of `repoRoot`. Existing `--trust-dir`/main-repository structural guards remain; this is not permission to treat a linked checkout as the Land destination.
- `mergeWorkflowBranch` inspects the pinned branch's paged merge-base diff with `renames:false` and gates before native Land. Integrate passes the admitted **recorded origin**, not main or a generated writer checkout. Even an already-integrated/no-op branch refuses corrupt authority. There remain only two Land prepare doors in these sources; the other Land instances are recovery/acknowledgement only.
- All admissions still call the existing lease module. No module/hook/helper/registry/baseline/skill/docs change, token transport, new configuration or infrastructure dependency was added. The operation-local result is a trusted **in-process** argument, never a saved authorization or new CLI bypass flag. Land is a separate effect door and refreshes authority once for each landing, even when its Workflow execution was admitted earlier.

### Finding → owning proof

“Closed” here means implemented with a passing production-path boundary proof on this branch, **not** independent acceptance or whole-suite green.

| Review scenario | Owning production-path proof |
| --- | --- |
| Linked holder cannot land | `test/runner/merge-land.test.mjs`: `a held documentation lease permits a code-only land and its holder can land scoped files` now acquires **only in a linked holder**, lands through the main-root API with explicit caller, and verifies `main:docs/new.md`. It also keeps corrupt/code-only refusal. `test/runner/promote-engine.test.mjs`: `retargetMember carries a linked holder checkout to the shared Land door` proves the forwarding boundary and non-holder refusal. |
| Workflow integrate bypass | `test/workflow/units-runner.test.mjs`: `workflow integration gates scoped branch paths before Land and admits a linked holder` proves unchanged main/WAL/candidate state on refusal, a real native holder land, and corrupt no-op refusal. `test/workflow/case-verbs.test.mjs`: `a holder workflow keeps its recorded checkout through resume, answer and integration from main` drives the actual integrate step after a main-cwd resume/answer; only the earlier settled writer output is seeded, not the integration. |
| Cwd-dependent bind/doctor | `test/runner/execution/bind.test.mjs`: `seat choice and doctor dry binds ignore a held documentation lease in every checkout` compares real bind and walk results, `checkExecutorPostureCoverage` and `checkSeatBindingDryBind` before/after acquire and from main/holder cwd. The former whole-bind-refusal test is **retired because that behavior was removed**; execution-door tests now own refusal. |
| Writing Unit replay from non-holder cwd | `test/runner/execution/run-case-admission.test.mjs`: existing documentation-admission test now uses a **writing** Unit and its real assignment/replay, main cwd, linked holder worktree, no replay lease refusal, one observed typed sidecar read, and corrupt refusal before another assignment exists. |
| Holder Workflow writer; resume/answer side effects | `test/workflow/units-runner.test.mjs`: `a holder workflow admits its writing unit using its origin, with one sidecar read for execution` reaches a real writing assignment in a token-free generated checkout and observes **one** native sidecar read across start, Unit and replay. `test/workflow/case-verbs.test.mjs`: `workflow resume and answer refuse a non-holder origin before any ledger, runtime or branch writes` covers foreground/detached resume/answer, even when later calls name the holder; it compares all `.fgos` file bytes plus refs, worktree registrations and Git status after **each** refusal. |
| Repeated consultations / stale comment | The two execution spies wrap and call through real `GitInspect.prototype.call`, counting only the sidecar's `pathsAtRevision`; no gate is mocked or skipped. Bind's held-lease walk proof covers later seat selection too. Root errors now stop before reclamation, as the corrected comment says. |

The writing boundary proofs assert real assignment/replay reachability, **not successful fake-worker completion**. Existing standalone/chain tests own completion, and remain red in this sandbox as disclosed below. This does not claim the sandbox executed every worker successfully. The lease module's independent token/corruption/owner-repair matrix remains its existing owning proof; this slice preserves the direct repair routing in `bin/fgos.mjs` without going through execution.

## L12-110 — Documentation lease contracts and pending token diagnostic (Doer, 2026-10-22)

### Supplied Lead rulings

Recorded from `step-06b-docs-brief.md`, not a new owner decision or code delivery:

1. **L-1, code follow-up:** doctor and acquire must report a stray holder-token copy left in the main checkout when main is not the holder worktree. The current linked-holder release lends the authenticated token to main under main-50 and restores/removes it in `finally`. SIGKILL/power loss can leave that copy. `checkDocumentationCutoverLeaseDoctor` currently checks wiring, main-ref state and lock-resource existence only; acquire currently does not inspect/report main's token. This docs-only turn records the gap and does not claim it fixed.
2. **Holder trust boundary:** the same OS user who can read the token file can act as holder. This explicitly applies to `landRunnerItem` as well as the other admission gates. The lease prevents accidents, not a deliberate same-user bypass. A main-50 borrower alone is not holder proof.

### Documentation delivered

- `docs/platform/holder-lock/spec.md` section 7 adds the rank-10 `documentation-cutover` row (lease/repo, fence none, owner documentation, null legacy fields, public acquire API only). Section 19 owns the complete maintainer contract: commands, fixed scope, committed-main authority, transition locks/ScopedCommit, admission doors, token custody, abandon/repair, drift, token-loss and crash limits. Section 20 records the dated D8 hardening-lane decision.
- `docs/platform/git/spec.md` section 7 places the lease guard before the at-home exemption and records the prepared/main shell filter, shared NUL-safe policy, legal borrowed sidecar edges, release-token proof, owner repair, raw-main refusal and fail-closed errors. It names both runner and Workflow Land gates for hook-disabled typed operations. Section 18 records the dated hardening decision.
- `docs/platform/packaging-distribution/contracts/setup-doctor-registry.md` adds `documentation-cutover-lease` in alphabetical order in the registered check inventory and records pass/fail/not-applicable behavior. It explicitly does not claim token inspection or an automatic fix.
- `CHANGELOG.md` adds one Unreleased entry covering verbs, hooks, doctor and execution/Land refusal.

### Code versus earlier design

Read design sections 3/6, L12-104 through L12-109 and the current lease/hook/CLI/registration/execution/Workflow/Land sources. Current code and the later rulings take precedence over the historical design:

- The sidecar is the plan-independent `plans/documentation-cutover-lease.json` committed on main, not a report-local or working-tree authority. Kernel handles serialize transitions only, in rank order 10 → 50; no TTL, callback lifetime or session identity controls the persistent freeze. Acquire by an existing holder refuses rather than renewing.
- Acquire validates the registered holder checkout and records pinned-main tree/blob OIDs, including absent roots. It does not enumerate dirty scoped worktrees or unmerged branches, compare a reviewed inventory, or run ratchet/rehearsal gates. Those remain cutover-operator obligations; no synthetic cleanliness evidence is claimed. It also does not itself check hook wiring, although doctor diagnoses that wiring.
- Release reports drift and still commits released state. It does not leave the lease held on drift or run the verification suite. The sidecar is excluded from its own digest. Phase 9 must independently recompute digests from Git and never trust `drift` or `releaseScopeDigests` as authority; the hooks authenticate transitions, not digest truth.
- Current scope adds the sidecar itself. The docs switchboard is already included under `docs/**`; the exact JSON switchboard remains a required frozen path, linked from the product contract without introducing a plan label in its prose. Other plan evidence stays outside scope.
- Current execution admission is at Unit and Workflow start/resume/answer doors, before effects, once per operation; `bind()`/dry seat choice/replay no longer consult the lease. Holder identity is the explicitly named checkout's token, never an ambient session id or token passed through env/argv/Unit records. Workflow keeps its recorded origin across generated writer checkouts. Both runner and Workflow Land authenticate at their own effect doors; code-only Land remains allowed. Corrupt authority refuses even code-only/no-op Land.
- Raw main ref moves into frozen scope refuse for holders too. Only sidecar-only, direct-parent, main-50-borrowed legal transitions are admitted by the hook. Release additionally needs the committing checkout's token. Explicit owner-confirmed abandon with a reason is deliberately available without a holder token or a holder-death test, including corrupt-sidecar repair; a busy kernel transition lock still refuses.
- The sh hook fast path avoids Node only for non-prepared phases or transactions without main. Main updates still depend on policy imports. The pre-commit free-state fast path skips path diffing. No separate per-writer API or direct-file-edit gate is asserted by these docs.
- Doctor is not applicable outside Git, without these repository hooks or without main. It does not inspect a linked holder's token; the stray-main-token diagnostic remains the L-1 code follow-up above.

## L12-111 — Repository-bound holder proof and token diagnostics (Lead under A42; Doer delivery, 2026-10-22)

### Supplied Lead rulings

Recorded from `step-06b-final-fix-brief.md`, not new owner decisions:

1. The one `leaseAdmission` gate reads authority from the gated `repoRoot`'s main ref. During a held lease the caller's main checkout, resolved by `resolveMainCheckoutRoot`, must equal that repository's main checkout. Foreign and non-Git callers cannot be holders; non-Git callers are refused for read-only work too. Keep the check shared by execution, Workflow operations and both Land doors.
2. Doctor fails precisely, and acquire refuses, on a token file in main when main is not the recorded holder checkout. Document the release-token lending crash window.
3. Fix contained L-3/L-4 issues; retain honest limits for corrupt-sidecar maintenance transactions, composite Work-item dispatch and legacy pre-case origins.
4. `units-runner`, `case-verbs` and `promote-engine` tests stage the addon themselves once at module level using the existing `ensureScriptAddon` helper.

### Delivery and owning proofs

- **Same-repository admission:** `src/runner/documentation-cutover-lease.mjs` resolves gated authority first, checks caller repository identity before reading its token or allowing an out-of-scope path set, and leaves the non-repository read-only exception available only for a genuinely non-repository `repoRoot`. No same-repository checks were copied into the four call sites. `run.mjs` changes only its explanatory exception comment. Existing no-lease admission and same-repository code-only Land remain unchanged.
  - Owning predicate proof: `test/runner/documentation-cutover-lease.test.mjs`, `admission reads the gated main and requires its held caller to belong to the same repository`. It covers foreign/non-Git callers, read-only and path-bearing asks, a copied token in a foreign repo, symlink/subdirectory holder identity, and corrupt gated authority that a free foreign repository must not hide.
  - Wiring proofs extend `run-case-admission.test.mjs`'s existing admission test (including read-only and resume), `units-runner.test.mjs`'s existing Workflow Land test, and `case-verbs.test.mjs`'s effect snapshot test (foreground/detached start, resume and answer, plus origins recorded before the freeze). `merge-land.test.mjs` adds one foreign/non-Git caller wiring proof. Both Lands preserve main, the operation directory and candidate absence on refusal. Workflow refuses foreign start before a ledger, and a later holder/main caller cannot upgrade a previously recorded foreign/non-Git origin. Existing positive holder integration remains covered.
- **Stray main token:** shared private predicate reused by doctor/acquire, with no token value in the error and no automatic deletion. The owning unit test models interrupted-release residue with the actual holder's token and checks unchanged main/token bytes. The existing failed-transition hook test now also proves the CLI refuses reacquire with a retained stray token; it then removes that fixture residue before testing an unrelated forced commit rejection.
- **Doctor with missing hook:** held/corrupt sidecar authority is read before deciding applicability. The existing doctor proof removes the reference hook and observes a failing message that still names the held lease. A plain consumer without a lease and without the reference hook still gets not-applicable.
- **Receipt failure after publication:** acquire's failure cleanup re-reads main before deleting its token. A matching held hash, or corrupt/unreadable committed evidence, keeps the token. The new owning test calls the real native ScopedCommit, corrupts only the returned receipt's file list, observes the reported failure, then proves the committed lease still has a usable holder token. Existing hook rejection/base-moved/concurrent-acquire tests retain rollback ownership.
- **Single-file staging:** the three named files import `../../scripts/lib/git-ports.mjs` and call `ensureScriptAddon()` once at module scope. No preload is required for current-tree tests; no new helper, production bypass, config default, environment key, dependency or registration was added. The existing doctor registration continues to own this diagnostic.
- **Generated census:** regenerated `packages/git/contracts/mutation-census.v1.json` with its existing generator. This also refreshes pre-existing stale hashes/line references left by the prior caller-admission delivery. Its 53 pre-existing sites are byte-equal to HEAD's site inventory; no site was added or suppressed. This is not a claim that the one-door scan is green.

Read both review reports completely. Their `scratchpad/6b2-r2/foreign.test.mjs` and `wf-foreign.test.mjs` files were not present in the checkout or accessible scratch locations; the committed reports supply the scenarios reproduced above. This delivery does not claim to have read or executed those unavailable original scripts.

Prior art: `git log -S'leaseAdmission'` identified `a5d09c37f` (declared caller identity) and `e8c1df31c` (initial gates); lease history includes `83a331410` (hook/recovery hardening). Reused the existing main-root resolver, native ScopedCommit, doctor registration, admission result threading and test staging helper; the missing piece was repository-bound authority, not another gate.

### Known limits retained, not claimed closed

- **L-2:** corrupt committed sidecars also refuse no-op main transactions such as `pack-refs --all`/`gc`. Owner-confirmed abandon/repair is still the recovery door; this fail-closed maintenance limit is unchanged.
- **N2:** composite Work-item dispatch can `pick` (claim/create worktree) before `runUnit` refuses. Claim worktrees do not hold the token, so a holder's Work-item dispatch through them is refused. No edit was made to the out-of-scope composite dispatch callers; this is distinct from the now-proven early refusal of the four assigned doors.
- **N3:** legacy pre-case runs resumed while no lease is held may record that resumer's worktree as origin on first case creation. During a held lease the pre-origin fallback is still main and cannot upgrade to holder. Changing case creation/binding semantics was not folded into this gate fix; the limitation remains explicit.
- Release still lends the token under main-50. SIGKILL/power loss can leave it in main before `finally`; doctor/acquire now diagnose/refuse the stray copy, but no automatic cleanup or hostile-same-uid protection is claimed. The accepted readable-token trust boundary and owner abandon remain unchanged.

- `docs/platform/holder-lock/spec.md`: admission reads the gated repository's main ref and requires a caller in the same repository; foreign/non-Git callers, including read-only, cannot hold its lease. Acquire refuses a token left in main when main is not the recorded holder; interrupted linked-holder release is a possible cause. Acquire keeps a token if a commit landed but its receipt failed validation (or committed evidence cannot be read).
- `docs/platform/git/spec.md`: both typed Land doors use this shared repository-bound caller check before publication; their hooks-disabled behavior does not accept another project's free sidecar as authority.
- `docs/platform/packaging-distribution/contracts/setup-doctor-registry.md`: doctor reports a held/corrupt lease even if the reference hook disappears, and fails precisely on a stray main token; no automatic token cleanup. Narrow the earlier no-hooks not-applicable statement to projects without lease authority/residue.
- Maintainer limits in those contracts should retain the three L-2/N2/N3 limits above, rather than implying universal pre-effect refusal or retroactive legacy-origin correction.
- `CHANGELOG.md` Unreleased follow-up: documentation lease admission is repository-bound, and doctor/acquire diagnose interrupted-release token residue.

## L12-113 — Stray main token is not admission proof (Lead under A42; Doer code delivery)

### Ruling, scope and delivery

Read `step-06b-o1-brief.md` and `review-a38-step-06b-final.md` completely. The supplied ruling closes O1 in the shared `leaseAdmission` gate only; O2 (recorded Workflow origin decides, not the later answerer's checkout) and O3 (`process.cwd()` fallback without `repoRoot`) remain unchanged for the next docs turn. No new owner ruling is asserted here.

`src/runner/documentation-cutover-lease.mjs` now canonicalizes the caller checkout once and reuses `hasStrayMainToken`: while authority is held, a main-checkout caller with a token but a different recorded holder checkout is refused with `allowed: false`, `holder: false`, reason `stray-main-token`. The check precedes token-hash admission and the out-of-scope paths exception. It does not delete the token or mutate authority. A linked holder is unaffected. Corrupt/free authority and the repository-membership check keep their prior order and semantics. No release, hook, caller, config, dependency or doctor-registration mechanism changed.

Prior art: `git log -S'hasStrayMainToken' -- src/runner/documentation-cutover-lease.mjs` identifies `2fbf9f018`, which added the shared doctor/acquire predicate but left admission accepting the copied token. The recent lease history also includes `83a331410` (hooks/recovery) and `8b20f5185` (ScopedCommit transitions). This change extends that existing gate/predicate rather than adding a parallel holder mechanism.

Owning proof: `test/runner/documentation-cutover-lease.test.mjs`, **a stray main token never grants admission while the linked holder can still release**. The older stray-token test owns doctor/acquire diagnosis, not admission. The new test acquires through production ScopedCommit, copies the real token to fixture main to model interrupted lending, and verifies main root/default/subdirectory/symlink callers refuse with and without scoped/code-only/empty paths; refusal does not leak the token, move main or remove its file. The real holder's subdirectory remains admitted and production release commits released state and deletes the holder token. Boundary files remain unchanged; no second copy of these cases was added there.

**Brief discrepancy — do not claim automatic residue cleanup:** the brief says holder release “removes the copy”, but the existing release `finally` deliberately restores *previous main bytes*. With a stray token already present, holder release succeeds and removes the holder's own token, but preserves the pre-existing main residue. The prior review explicitly reported this same behavior under L-1, and the current holder-lock spec states it. Changing release would contradict the brief's “one contained change in the gate” restriction; this delivery therefore leaves release unchanged. The owning test asserts that actual preservation, then explicitly removes the fixture residue as an operator action. Automatic deletion of pre-existing main residue is **not delivered** and needs a clarified Lead ruling if intended; no extra write scope is needed for that separate source change.

## L12-114 — Documentation cutover lease rehearsal on disposable clone (Doer delivery, 2026-10-22)

### Rehearsal execution and findings

Owner decision 6 and Lead brief `step-06b-rehearsal-brief.md` were executed end-to-end against a disposable clone at `/tmp/fgos-work/lease-rehearsal/repo` on branch `main` at commit `5dd5feeb41287623dd9d7e208b8922f47ab8d9a6`, with linked worktrees `/tmp/fgos-work/lease-rehearsal/holder` (`holder`) and `/tmp/fgos-work/lease-rehearsal/other` (`other`). All seven rehearsal steps were executed in sequence; every step passed without code modifications:

1. **Initial status and doctor check:** `docs-lease status --json` reported `{state: "free", held: false, sidecar: null}` (exit 0). `doctor` check `documentation-cutover-lease` reported `passed: true` (`"no documentation-cutover lease is held; reference-transaction hook wired"`).
2. **Acquisition:** `docs-lease acquire --worktree <holder> --json` acquired the lease and committed the held sidecar to `main` (`a8eef9f71`, `chore(docs): acquired documentation cutover lease`, base `5dd5feeb4`). The token was written exclusively to `/tmp/fgos-work/lease-rehearsal/holder/.fgos/documentation-cutover.token` (permissions `-rw-------`, 65 bytes). Neither CLI stdout nor the committed `plans/documentation-cutover-lease.json` contains the plaintext token; only the SHA-256 digest (`9f3bfbb3...`) is recorded.
3. **Refusals:**
   - *Pre-commit hook:* A non-holder commit touching `docs/refusal-test.md` in `other` was refused with `commit refused: the documentation-cutover write lease is held; non-holder commits to scoped paths are refused` (exit 1).
   - *Reference-transaction hook:* The same scoped commit created on `other` with `--no-verify` followed by `git update-ref refs/heads/main HEAD` was aborted by the `reference-transaction` hook with `reference-transaction refused: reference update touches documentation scope or an unauthorized lease transition; failing closed` (exit 128), leaving `main` unchanged at `a8eef9f71`.
   - *Execution admission:* Non-holder `fgos run "read docs" --json` in `other` was refused before dispatch side effects with `fgos: documentation cutover lease is held; non-holder operations are refused` (canonical exit 4, `policy-refusal`).
   - *Runner Land door:* Non-holder land of scoped branch `other` via `landRunnerItem` threw `MergeError` (`documentation-cutover-lease`, `"documentation cutover lease is held; non-holder operations are refused"`) before candidate creation, leaving `main` unchanged and `operationId: null`.
   - *Foreign checkout:* A caller from an unrelated temporary `git init` repo calling `landRunnerItem` threw `MergeError` (`documentation-cutover-lease`, `"documentation cutover lease is held; caller checkout must belong to the gated repository"`).
4. **Allowed operations:**
   - *Out-of-scope non-holder commit:* A commit touching `src/rehearsal-allowed.txt` in `other` was admitted by the pre-commit hook (commit `250caf632`, exit 0).
   - *Holder scoped commit:* A commit touching `docs/guide.md` in `holder` was admitted by the pre-commit hook via valid holder token (commit `5cff9477a`, exit 0).
   - *Holder Land door:* `landRunnerItem` targeting `main` with `callerCheckout: holder` landed cleanly (`LAND SUCCESS! Outcome: merged`), publishing merge commit `48b1ee21a` on `main`.
5. **Release:** `docs-lease release --json` from `holder` committed the released sidecar on `main` (`ac0c0dc58`, `chore(docs): released documentation cutover lease`), reported `drift: ["docs"]`, and automatically deleted the holder token file `/tmp/fgos-work/lease-rehearsal/holder/.fgos/documentation-cutover.token`.
6. **Re-acquire, abandon, and final cycle:**
   - Re-acquire succeeded (`fb4509f2c`, `chore(docs): acquired documentation cutover lease`).
   - Owner abandon with `--owner-confirmed --reason "rehearsal"` succeeded (`8b383aab7`, `chore(docs): abandoned documentation cutover lease`), recording the confirmed reason and holder hash in the sidecar.
   - Re-acquire after abandon succeeded (`0a033952a`, `chore(docs): acquired documentation cutover lease`).
   - Final release from `holder` succeeded (`900272044`, `chore(docs): released documentation cutover lease`, `drift: []`).
7. **Rollback & cleanliness proof:**
   - `git log --oneline main` confirms exactly the 8 authorized commits (`a8eef9f71`, `5cff9477a`, `48b1ee21a`, `ac0c0dc58`, `fb4509f2c`, `8b383aab7`, `0a033952a`, `900272044`) on top of input commit `5dd5feeb4`.
   - Refused operations left zero candidate worktrees, lingering lock handles, or operations residue.
   - The entire scratch directory `/tmp/fgos-work/lease-rehearsal` and state dir `/tmp/fgos-work/state` were removed at the end of the rehearsal.

### Rehearsal specifics and boundary conditions

- **Sparse checkout:** The clone used `--no-cone` sparse checkout excluding `plans/260925-documentation-authority-unification/reports/**` to avoid exhausting the 7.7GB `tmpfs` mounted at `/tmp` (which holds 6.4GB of historical report files if fully checked out across multiple worktrees).
- **State root & addon descriptor:** Under the read-only sandbox mount of `/home/vantt/.local/state/fgos`, `FGOS_STATE_ROOT` was directed to `/tmp/fgos-work/state` (mode 0700) to permit GitDriver's runtime floor probe, and `native-addon.json` was placed in the disposable clone to resolve the native addon for the git hook environment where `FGOS_ACTIVE_MANIFEST_PATH` is scrubbed by Git.
- **Token privacy:** The holder token is generated via `crypto.randomBytes(32)` at mode 0600 in the holder's `.fgos/`; neither CLI JSON envelopes nor committed sidecar bytes expose the plaintext token (only its SHA-256 hash).
- **Scope:** No git write ran in this repository; no installed `fgos` CLI, workspace `.fgos` access, secrets read, or lifecycle verbs were used. Deliverable files are `reports/phase-08/step-06b-rehearsal.md` and this note. `needsWrites: []`.
